I’m not sure if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I think about two years now—maybe two years and a couple months. It kind of started after this dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. I don’t know if that sounds like a clear starting point, but that’s when I started noticing it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some things I used to like, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. I guess maybe they don’t always feel the same, but… I don’t know, maybe that’s normal? Like, I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, and I can get myself to go along with stuff. It just maybe feels a little less… rewarding? But I’m not sure if that counts, or if I’m making too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, at least sometimes. Like, if something is supposed to be fun, I can usually recognize that it’s nice, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. It’s just maybe not as strong as I’d expect? I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I don’t.” It’s more like… some things feel a little flatter than they used to, maybe. But I still can enjoy things at least somewhat, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I think about two years now—maybe two years and a couple months. It kind of started after this dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. I don’t know if that sounds like a clear starting point, but that’s when I started noticing it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some things I used to like, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. I guess maybe they don’t always feel the same, but… I don’t know, maybe that’s normal? Like, I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, and I can get myself to go along with stuff. It just maybe feels a little less… rewarding? But I’m not sure if that counts, or if I’m making too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, at least sometimes. Like, if something is supposed to be fun, I can usually recognize that it’s nice, and I don’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good anymore. It’s just maybe not as strong as I’d expect? I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I don’t.” It’s more like… some things feel a little flatter than they used to, maybe. But I still can enjoy things at least somewhat, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, even if you still participate in them?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure I’d call it “lost pleasure.” I still show up and do things, and sometimes they’re okay. It’s more like I’m going through the motions a bit, or the enjoyment is kind of muted. But I don’t want to overstate it if that’s not what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that you do where you expect to feel happy, but the happiness isn't as strong as you think it should be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a fair way to put it. Like, I’ll expect something to feel really good, and then when I’m there it’s just… fine. Not bad, exactly, just not as satisfying as I thought it would be. I still do feel some happiness sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like it’s completely gone. But maybe it’s a little weaker than it should be, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt completely back to normal for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might have been better stretches here and there, but I don’t think I’ve had a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. It’s been kind of ongoing for about two years… maybe a little over, like two years and two months, since that dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a specific thing and say, “I used to love that and now I don’t.” It’s more like… some things feel a little flatter than they used to, maybe. But I still can enjoy things at least somewhat, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, even if you still participate in them?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure I’d call it “lost pleasure.” I still show up and do things, and sometimes they’re okay. It’s more like I’m going through the motions a bit, or the enjoyment is kind of muted. But I don’t want to overstate it if that’s not what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that you do where you expect to feel happy, but the happiness isn't as strong as you think it should be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a fair way to put it. Like, I’ll expect something to feel really good, and then when I’m there it’s just… fine. Not bad, exactly, just not as satisfying as I thought it would be. I still do feel some happiness sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like it’s completely gone. But maybe it’s a little weaker than it should be, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt completely back to normal for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might have been better stretches here and there, but I don’t think I’ve had a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. It’s been kind of ongoing for about two years… maybe a little over, like two years and two months, since that dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my weight probably goes up or down a little like anyone’s, but I haven’t noticed anything significant over the past couple years. But I don’t want to overstate it if that’s not what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that you do where you expect to feel happy, but the happiness isn't as strong as you think it should be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a fair way to put it. Like, I’ll expect something to feel really good, and then when I’m there it’s just… fine. Not bad, exactly, just not as satisfying as I thought it would be. I still do feel some happiness sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like it’s completely gone. But maybe it’s a little weaker than it should be, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt completely back to normal for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might have been better stretches here and there, but I don’t think I’ve had a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. It’s been kind of ongoing for about two years… maybe a little over, like two years and two months, since that dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my weight probably goes up or down a little like anyone’s, but I haven’t noticed anything significant over the past couple years. At least nothing that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt really down or depressed, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days I’ve had this kind of flat, heavy feeling. Like even when work is going okay, or people are being nice to me, I still feel down or kind of empty underneath. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt really down or depressed for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say. I think about two years now—maybe two years and a couple months. It kind of started after this dashboard project at work put me more in the spotlight. I don’t know if that sounds like a clear starting point, but that’s when I started noticing it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some things I used to like, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. I guess maybe they don’t always feel the same, but… I don’t know, maybe that’s normal? But I still can enjoy things at least somewhat, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.
